Lecture Goals: From Physics to Policy

The PushT simulator from the dynamics lecture becomes a training ground

Physicsimpulse · friction · walls
Demonstrationshuman or scripted expert
ImitationMLP-BC · Diffusion Policy
RL fine-tuningPPO, started from BC

Three questions answered with experiments in this lecture

Q1 — Data : how many demonstrations do we need to collect?

Q2 — Model : which network should learn from them — a regressor or a generative model?

Q3 — Beyond imitation : can reinforcement learning do better, from scratch or starting from the imitation policy?

Pipeline : demonstrations D={(ot,at)}\mathcal D=\{(o_t,a_t)\}D={(ot​,at​)} → dataset → policy πϕ(a∣o)\pi_\phi(\mathbf a\mid o)πϕ​(a∣o) → closed-loop success rate on unseen tasks

Control Loop and Demonstration Data

The policy moves the pusher's target at 10 Hz; the physics runs 20 substeps in between

State (8 numbers — what a human sees)

st=(xP, yP⏟pusher, xG, yG, θ⏟T, xg, yg, θg⏟goal)s_t=\big(\underbrace{x_P,\,y_P}_{\text{pusher}},\ \underbrace{x_G,\,y_G,\,\theta}_{\text{T}},\ \underbrace{x_g,\,y_g,\,\theta_g}_{\text{goal}}\big)st​=(pusherxP​,yP​​​, TxG​,yG​,θ​​, goalxg​,yg​,θg​​​)

Action

at=(x∗, y∗)  = pusher target,Δt=0.1 sa_t=(x^{*},\,y^{*})\ \ \text{= pusher target},\qquad \Delta t=0.1\ \text{s}at​=(x∗,y∗)  = pusher target,Δt=0.1 s

A PD controller pulls the pusher toward the target; contact with the T is the impulse model of the dynamics lecture.

One episode, saved to .npz

τ=((s0,a0),(s1,a1),…,(sT−1,aT−1))\tau=\big((s_0,a_0),(s_1,a_1),\dots,(s_{T-1},a_{T-1})\big)τ=((s0​,a0​),(s1​,a1​),…,(sT−1​,aT−1​))
ArrayShapeMeaning
states(N, 8)sts_tst​
actions(N, 2)ata_tat​ = target
episode_ends(E,)where each episode ends
seeds(E,)re-creates the start

Only successful episodes are saved : imitating a failure teaches failure (filtered behavior cloning). Same format as the Diffusion Policy / LeRobot PushT data.

Physics Inside One Control Step

One env.step() = 0.1 s = 20 substeps of 5 ms, each with a 3-pass contact solver

Targetat=(x∗,y∗)a_t=(x^{*},y^{*})at​=(x∗,y∗)
PD pusherxP, vP\mathbf x_P,\ \mathbf v_PxP​, vP​
Contactpoint, n\mathbf nn, depth
Impulsejn, jtj_n,\ j_tjn​, jt​
T motion(vG,ω)→(xG,θ)(\mathbf v_G,\omega)\to(\mathbf x_G,\theta)(vG​,ω)→(xG​,θ)

Time steps

nsub=round(ΔtctrlΔtphys)=0.10.005=20n_{\text{sub}}=\mathrm{round}\Big(\frac{\Delta t_{\text{ctrl}}}{\Delta t_{\text{phys}}}\Big)=\frac{0.1}{0.005}=20nsub​=round(Δtphys​Δtctrl​​)=0.0050.1​=20

Average contact force

F≈1Δtctrl∑substepsJ,J=∫F dt\mathbf F\approx\frac{1}{\Delta t_{\text{ctrl}}}\sum_{\text{substeps}}\mathbf J,\qquad \mathbf J=\int\mathbf F\,dtF≈Δtctrl​1​substeps∑​J,J=∫Fdt

pusht_imitation.py : PushTEnv.step · pusht_keyboard_sim.py : advance

def step(self, action):

self.sim.target = np.clip(action, [x_min, y_min], [x_max, y_max])

self.sim.advance(self.control_dt) # 0.1 s

self.t += 1

def advance(self, frame_dt):

n = max(1, int(round(frame_dt / self.physics_dt)))

self._j_sum[:] = 0.0

for _ in range(n):

self.substep(self.physics_dt) # 5 ms

F = self._j_sum / (n * self.physics_dt) # impulse / time

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
5

Substep: PD Pusher, Damping, Integration

The pusher is a controlled kinematic body; the T coasts and slows down by floor friction

1. PD control of the pusher

aP=Kp(x∗−xP)−Kd vP\mathbf a_P=K_p(\mathbf x^{*}-\mathbf x_P)-K_d\,\mathbf v_PaP​=Kp​(x∗−xP​)−Kd​vP​

Kp=80, Kd=18K_p=80,\ K_d=18Kp​=80, Kd​=18 : damping ratio ζ=Kd/(2Kp)≈1\zeta=K_d/(2\sqrt{K_p})\approx1ζ=Kd​/(2Kp​​)≈1 — fast, no overshoot. Speed limited to 333 m/s.

2. Floor friction as exponential decay

vG←vG e−cvΔt,ω←ω e−cωΔt\mathbf v_G\leftarrow\mathbf v_G\,e^{-c_v\Delta t},\qquad \omega\leftarrow\omega\,e^{-c_\omega\Delta t}vG​←vG​e−cv​Δt,ω←ωe−cω​Δt

3. Semi-implicit Euler

xG←xG+vGΔt,θ←θ+ωΔt\mathbf x_G\leftarrow\mathbf x_G+\mathbf v_G\Delta t,\qquad \theta\leftarrow\theta+\omega\Delta txG​←xG​+vG​Δt,θ←θ+ωΔt

pusht_keyboard_sim.py : KeyboardPushT.substep

# 1. pusher PD control toward the target

acc = self.kp * (self.target - P.pos) - self.kd * P.vel

P.vel += acc * dt

speed = np.linalg.norm(P.vel)

if speed > self.max_speed:

P.vel *= self.max_speed / speed

P.pos += P.vel * dt # new velocity first

# 2. damping 3. integrate the T

T.vel *= math.exp(-self.linear_damping * dt)

T.omega *= math.exp(-self.angular_damping * dt)

T.pos += T.vel * dt

T.angle += T.omega * dt

Consequence : released at speed vvv, the T slides another ∫0∞ve−cvtdt=v/cv\int_0^\infty v e^{-c_v t}dt=v/c_v∫0∞​ve−cv​tdt=v/cv​ — the expert slows down near the goal for this reason.

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
6

T Geometry: Mass Moment of Inertia

Composite body: two rectangles, each shifted to the T's centre of mass

Centre of mass (mass ∝ area)

yˉ=A1y1+A2y2A1+A2\bar y=\frac{A_1y_1+A_2y_2}{A_1+A_2}yˉ​=A1​+A2​A1​y1​+A2​y2​​

Rectangle + parallel-axis theorem

IG=∑i[mi12(ai2+bi2)+mi di2]I_G=\sum_i\Big[\tfrac{m_i}{12}(a_i^2+b_i^2)+m_i\,d_i^2\Big]IG​=i∑​[12mi​​(ai2​+bi2​)+mi​di2​]
Value (m=1m=1m=1)Result
top bar1.8×0.51.8\times0.51.8×0.5
stem0.5×1.30.5\times1.30.5×1.3
COM below the top edge0.627
IGI_GIG​0.434

pusht_keyboard_sim.py : make_T_geometry

top_area = W * top_t

stem_h = H - top_t

stem_area = stem_w * stem_h

total_area = top_area + stem_area

y_top, y_stem = H / 2 - top_t / 2, -top_t / 2

y_com = (top_area * y_top + stem_area * y_stem) / total_area

m_top = mass * top_area / total_area

m_stem = mass * stem_area / total_area

# own centre + parallel-axis shift to the COM

I_top = m_top * (W**2 + top_t**2) / 12 \

+ m_top * (y_top - y_com) ** 2

I_stem = m_stem * (stem_w**2 + stem_h**2) / 12 \

+ m_stem * (y_stem - y_com) ** 2

vertices[:, 1] -= y_com # body origin = COM

7

Contact Detection: Circle vs T

Work in the T's own frame: closest point on the 8 edges, then inside / outside

World → body frame

c=R(θ) ⁣⊤(xP−xG)\mathbf c=R(\theta)^{\!\top}(\mathbf x_P-\mathbf x_G)c=R(θ)⊤(xP​−xG​)

Closest point on edge iii

ti=clip((c−ai)⋅(bi−ai)∥bi−ai∥2,0,1)t_i=\mathrm{clip}\Big(\frac{(\mathbf c-\mathbf a_i)\cdot(\mathbf b_i-\mathbf a_i)}{\lVert\mathbf b_i-\mathbf a_i\rVert^2},0,1\Big)ti​=clip(∥bi​−ai​∥2(c−ai​)⋅(bi​−ai​)​,0,1)
qi=ai+ti(bi−ai),i∗=arg⁡min⁡i∥qi−c∥\mathbf q_i=\mathbf a_i+t_i(\mathbf b_i-\mathbf a_i),\quad i^{*}=\arg\min_i\lVert\mathbf q_i-\mathbf c\rVertqi​=ai​+ti​(bi​−ai​),i∗=argimin​∥qi​−c∥

Penetration (pusher radius ρ\rhoρ)

δ={ρ−doutsideρ+dinside,n=R nlocal\delta=\begin{cases}\rho-d & \text{outside}\\ \rho+d & \text{inside}\end{cases},\qquad \mathbf n=R\,\mathbf n_{\text{local}}δ={ρ−dρ+d​outsideinside​,n=Rnlocal​

pusht_imitation.py : circle_vs_T_fast (all edges at once)

R = rotation_matrix(body_angle)

c = R.T @ (circle_pos - body_pos) # world -> T

A = vertices

AB = np.roll(vertices, -1, axis=0) - A

t = np.clip(((c - A) * AB).sum(1) / (AB * AB).sum(1), 0, 1)

Q = A + t[:, None] * AB # closest points

d2 = ((Q - c) ** 2).sum(1)

i = int(np.argmin(d2))

d = math.sqrt(d2[i])

inside = ... # ray casting

if not inside and d >= radius:

return None

direction = (Q[i] - c) / d

n_local, pen = (-direction, radius + d) if inside \

else (direction, radius - d)

n = R @ n_local

return body_pos + R @ Q[i], n, pen

Speed : the numpy version replaces an 8-iteration Python loop — identical results on 2000 random cases, checked in the tutorial.

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
8

Contact Velocity and Normal Impulse

Relative velocity says whether the bodies approach; an impulse stops them

Velocity of the contact point

vC=vG+ω×r,ω×r=(−ωry, ωrx)\mathbf v_C=\mathbf v_G+\boldsymbol\omega\times\mathbf r,\qquad \boldsymbol\omega\times\mathbf r=(-\omega r_y,\ \omega r_x)vC​=vG​+ω×r,ω×r=(−ωry​, ωrx​)
vn=(vC−vP)⋅n <0  ⇒ approachingv_n=(\mathbf v_C-\mathbf v_P)\cdot\mathbf n\ <0\ \ \Rightarrow\ \text{approaching}vn​=(vC​−vP​)⋅n <0  ⇒ approaching

Normal impulse

Kn=1m+(r×n)2I,jn=−(1+e) vnKnK_n=\frac1m+\frac{(\mathbf r\times\mathbf n)^2}{I},\quad j_n=-\frac{(1+e)\,v_n}{K_n}Kn​=m1​+I(r×n)2​,jn​=−Kn​(1+e)vn​​
ΔvG=jnnm,Δω=r×jnnI\Delta\mathbf v_G=\frac{j_n\mathbf n}{m},\qquad \Delta\omega=\frac{\mathbf r\times j_n\mathbf n}{I}ΔvG​=mjn​n​,Δω=Ir×jn​n​

pusht_keyboard_sim.py : resolve_impulse (part 1)

point, n, pen = contact

r = point - body.pos

v_rel = body.vel + omega_cross_r(body.omega, r) - other_vel

v_n = float(np.dot(v_rel, n))

if v_n < 0: # approaching

K_n = 1.0 / body.mass + cross2(r, n) ** 2 / body.inertia

j_n = -(1.0 + restitution) * v_n / K_n

J = j_n * n

body.vel += J / body.mass

body.omega += cross2(r, J) / body.inertia

def cross2(a, b): # 2D cross product, z only

return a[0] * b[1] - a[1] * b[0]

Kinematic pusher : 1/mP=0, 1/IP=01/m_P=0,\ 1/I_P=01/mP​=0, 1/IP​=0, so only the T's terms remain in KnK_nKn​. PushT uses e=0e=0e=0 — after impact vn+=0v_n^{+}=0vn+​=0.

Why Contact Height Matters

The same push at a different height splits into translation and rotation differently

Derivation: normal velocity change at C

Δvn=(jnm+Δω×r)⋅n=j(1m+(r×n)2I)=jKn\Delta v_n=\Big(\frac{j\mathbf n}{m}+\Delta\boldsymbol\omega\times\mathbf r\Big)\cdot\mathbf n=j\Big(\frac1m+\frac{(\mathbf r\times\mathbf n)^2}{I}\Big)=jK_nΔvn​=(mjn​+Δω×r)⋅n=j(m1​+I(r×n)2​)=jKn​
vn+=vn+jKn=−e vn ⇒ j=−(1+e)vnKnv_n^{+}=v_n+jK_n=-e\,v_n\ \Rightarrow\ j=-\frac{(1+e)v_n}{K_n}vn+​=vn​+jKn​=−evn​ ⇒ j=−Kn​(1+e)vn​​

Stem's left face (x=−0.25x=-0.25x=−0.25), vP=(1,0)\mathbf v_P=(1,0)vP​=(1,0), e=0, μ=0e=0,\ \mu=0e=0, μ=0 :  r×n=−y,  Kn=1+y2/I\ \mathbf r\times\mathbf n=-y,\ \ K_n=1+y^2/I r×n=−y,  Kn​=1+y2/I

Height yyyKnK_nKn​jnj_njn​Δvx\Delta v_xΔvx​Δω\Delta\omegaΔω
0 (COM)1.0001.0001.0000.000
−0.51.5760.6340.6340.731
−1.03.3050.3030.3030.697

Output of resolve_impulse() — pusht_mini_sim.py, section 8

At the COM : pure translation. Far from the COM : smaller impulse, mostly rotation.

This is PushT : choosing the contact point chooses the motion — exactly what the scripted expert and the learned policies must do.

Coulomb Friction Impulse

Cancel the sliding velocity at the contact, but never beyond μ jn\mu\,j_nμjn​

Tangent direction

n=(nx,ny) ⇒ t=(−ny, nx)\mathbf n=(n_x,n_y)\ \Rightarrow\ \mathbf t=(-n_y,\ n_x)n=(nx​,ny​) ⇒ t=(−ny​, nx​)

Impulse that stops sliding

vt=vrel⋅t,Kt=1m+(r×t)2Iv_t=\mathbf v_{\text{rel}}\cdot\mathbf t,\qquad K_t=\frac1m+\frac{(\mathbf r\times\mathbf t)^2}{I}vt​=vrel​⋅t,Kt​=m1​+I(r×t)2​
jt∗=−vtKtj_t^{*}=-\frac{v_t}{K_t}jt∗​=−Kt​vt​​

Coulomb limit

jt=clip(jt∗, −μjn, μjn),μ=0.4j_t=\mathrm{clip}\big(j_t^{*},\,-\mu j_n,\,\mu j_n\big),\qquad \mu=0.4jt​=clip(jt∗​,−μjn​,μjn​),μ=0.4

pusht_keyboard_sim.py : resolve_impulse (part 2)

# friction, using the velocity after the normal impulse

t = np.array([-n[1], n[0]])

v_rel = body.vel + omega_cross_r(body.omega, r) - other_vel

v_t = float(np.dot(v_rel, t))

K_t = 1.0 / body.mass + cross2(r, t) ** 2 / body.inertia

j_t = float(np.clip(-v_t / K_t,

-friction * j_n, friction * j_n))

Jt = j_t * t

body.vel += Jt / body.mass

body.omega += cross2(r, Jt) / body.inertia

Effect : with friction the pusher can drag the T sideways and rotate it; with μ=0\mu=0μ=0 it slips along the surface (try F in the keyboard demo).

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
11

Walls and Penetration Correction

A wall is a kinematic body with zero velocity — the same impulse function handles it

Wall = pusher with v=0\mathbf v=\mathbf 0v=0

vother=0,1mwall=0\mathbf v_{\text{other}}=\mathbf 0,\qquad \frac1{m_{\text{wall}}}=0vother​=0,mwall​1​=0

Left wall:  δ=xmin⁡−pi,x>0,  n=(1,0)\ \delta=x_{\min}-p_{i,x}>0,\ \ \mathbf n=(1,0) δ=xmin​−pi,x​>0,  n=(1,0)

Penetration correction

xG←xG+0.8 max⁡(δ−10−4,0) n\mathbf x_G\leftarrow\mathbf x_G+0.8\,\max(\delta-10^{-4},0)\,\mathbf nxG​←xG​+0.8max(δ−10−4,0)n

Discrete time jumps t→t+Δtt\to t+\Delta tt→t+Δt leave overlaps that impulses alone cannot remove.

pusht_keyboard_sim.py : wall_contacts · substep (step 4)

world = body.pos + body.vertices @ rotation_matrix(body.angle).T

for p in world:

for depth, n in ((x_min - p[0], (1.0, 0.0)), ...):

if depth > 0:

contacts.append((p.copy(), np.array(n), depth))

# 4. contact solver: pusher-T and wall-T

for _ in range(3):

c = circle_vs_T(P.pos, P.radius, T.pos, T.angle, T.vertices)

if c is not None:

jn, jt = resolve_impulse(T, P.vel, c, e, mu)

for w in wall_contacts(T):

resolve_impulse(T, np.zeros(2), w, e, mu)

# end of resolve_impulse

body.pos += 0.8 * max(pen - 1e-4, 0.0) * n

Physics Summary: Equation ↔ Code

Every line of the simulator is an equation from the dynamics lecture

Lecture ideaEquationCode
PD controlaP=Kp(x∗−xP)−KdvP\mathbf a_P=K_p(\mathbf x^{*}-\mathbf x_P)-K_d\mathbf v_PaP​=Kp​(x∗−xP​)−Kd​vP​substep, step 1
Parallel-axis theoremIG=∑[mi12(ai2+bi2)+midi2]I_G=\sum\big[\tfrac{m_i}{12}(a_i^2+b_i^2)+m_id_i^2\big]IG​=∑[12mi​​(ai2​+bi2​)+mi​di2​]make_T_geometry
Relative velocityvC=vG+ω×r\mathbf v_C=\mathbf v_G+\boldsymbol\omega\times\mathbf rvC​=vG​+ω×romega_cross_r
Restitutionvn+=−e vn−v_n^{+}=-e\,v_n^{-}vn+​=−evn−​j_n = -(1+e) v_n / K_n
Linear impulse–momentumΔvG=J/m\Delta\mathbf v_G=\mathbf J/mΔvG​=J/mbody.vel += J / m
Angular impulse–momentumΔω=(r×J)/I\Delta\omega=(\mathbf r\times\mathbf J)/IΔω=(r×J)/Ibody.omega += cross2(r, J) / I
Coulomb friction∣jt∣≤μjn\lvert j_t\rvert\le\mu j_n∣jt​∣≤μjn​np.clip(-v_t / K_t, ...)
Impulse = force × timeF≈∑J/Δt\mathbf F\approx\sum\mathbf J/\Delta tF≈∑J/Δtadvance

13

Collecting Human Demonstrations

python pusht_imitation.py collect — the keyboard simulator with a recorder added

Recorder window: arrows / WASD or mouse drag move the target; reaching the goal auto-saves

Three rules for clean data

Fixed 10 Hz : the screen draws at 20 fps, but (st,at)(s_t,a_t)(st​,at​) is recorded every 0.1 s — one human step = one env.step().

Idle start trimmed : time spent thinking before the first move is not recorded, so the policy never learns to stand still.

Physics fixed : friction / restitution switches are off — same state, same consequence.

Keys : N new task · R restart · Backspace delete last · Space pause

Cost : one human demo takes 15–30 s, so 200 demos take 1.5–2 hours → we also need an automatic demonstrator.

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
14

Scripted Expert: Impulse–Momentum as a Policy

A "virtual human" that chooses where to push with the lecture's impulse equations

(1) Where to push — unit impulse nk\mathbf n_knk​ at candidate CkC_kCk​

ΔvG=nkm,Δω=rk×nkI\Delta\mathbf v_G=\frac{\mathbf n_k}{m},\qquad \Delta\omega=\frac{\mathbf r_k\times\mathbf n_k}{I}ΔvG​=mnk​​,Δω=Irk​×nk​​
k∗=arg⁡max⁡k ck⋅d∥ck∥ ∥d∥k^{*}=\arg\max_k\ \frac{\mathbf c_k\cdot\mathbf d}{\lVert\mathbf c_k\rVert\,\lVert\mathbf d\rVert}k∗=argkmax​ ∥ck​∥∥d∥ck​⋅d​

Candidates : 8 edges × 3 points; d\mathbf dd = desired (Δx,Δy,L Δθ)(\Delta x,\Delta y,L\,\Delta\theta)(Δx,Δy,LΔθ), ck\mathbf c_kck​ = its effect.

(2) Approach : go to a standoff point; orbit around the T if the straight path is blocked.

(3) Push : along nk\mathbf n_knk​, slowing in proportion to the remaining error (the T slides ≈v/cv\approx v/c_v≈v/cv​ after release).

Expert rollouts (orange: pusher path, colour: time) — success ≈ 88–91 % on random tasks
Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
15

Scripted Expert: Equation ↔ Code

Score each candidate push by how well its impulse response points toward the goal

Desired change (L=0.8L=0.8L=0.8)

d=(xg−xG, yg−yG, L(θg−θ))\mathbf d=\big(x_g-x_G,\ y_g-y_G,\ L(\theta_g-\theta)\big)d=(xg​−xG​, yg​−yG​, L(θg​−θ))

Response to a unit impulse at CkC_kCk​

ck=(nkm, L rk×nkI)\mathbf c_k=\Big(\frac{\mathbf n_k}{m},\ L\,\frac{\mathbf r_k\times\mathbf n_k}{I}\Big)ck​=(mnk​​, LIrk​×nk​​)

Cosine score

sk=ck⋅d∥ck∥∥d∥s_k=\frac{\mathbf c_k\cdot\mathbf d}{\lVert\mathbf c_k\rVert\lVert\mathbf d\rVert}sk​=∥ck​∥∥d∥ck​⋅d​

Penalty for pushing into a touching wall; +0.1 for keeping the same contact.

pusht_imitation.py : ScriptedExpert.act

e = sim.goal_pos - T.pos

d = np.array([e[0], e[1],

self.L * wrap_angle(sim.goal_angle - T.angle)])

for k, (c_local, out_local) in enumerate(self.cands):

cw, out = T.pos + R @ c_local, R @ out_local

n_in, r = -out, cw - T.pos

c = np.array([n_in[0] / T.mass, n_in[1] / T.mass,

self.L * cross2(r, n_in) / T.inertia])

score = c @ d / (np.linalg.norm(c) * d_norm + 1e-9)

for n_wall in walls:

if n_in @ np.array(n_wall) < -0.3:

score -= 0.6 # into that wall

if k == self.cur:

score += 0.1 # hysteresis

# push: slower when close to the goal

step = float(np.clip(0.3 * d_norm, 0.04, 0.12))

return P.pos - out * (step + 0.04)

How Many Demonstrations? Reference Points

Published PushT and manipulation work uses 50–200 demonstrations per task

SourceTaskDemonstrations
Diffusion Policy (Chi et al., 2023)PushT, fixed goal, random start200 human (≈ 25k steps)
LeRobot pusht datasetsame task206 episodes, 25,650 frames
ACT / ALOHA (Zhao et al., 2023)real bimanual robot50 per task

Suggested plan for human demonstrations (15–30 s each)

More important than the count : spread-out initial conditions, a consistent strategy, and discarded failures. Our goal is random too — harder than the original PushT.

Goal Frame: Relative Motion Removes the Random Goal

Ride along with the goal — the same idea as relative motion in rigid-body kinematics

p~=R(θg) ⁣⊤(p−xg),θ~=θ−θg\tilde{\mathbf p}=R(\theta_g)^{\!\top}(\mathbf p-\mathbf x_g),\qquad \tilde\theta=\theta-\theta_gp~​=R(θg​)⊤(p−xg​),θ~=θ−θg​
ot=(x~P, y~P, x~G, y~G, cos⁡θ~, sin⁡θ~)o_t=\big(\tilde x_P,\ \tilde y_P,\ \tilde x_G,\ \tilde y_G,\ \cos\tilde\theta,\ \sin\tilde\theta\big)ot​=(x~P​, y~​P​, x~G​, y~​G​, cosθ~, sinθ~)
a~t+i=R(θg) ⁣⊤(at+i−xP(t))\tilde{\mathbf a}_{t+i}=R(\theta_g)^{\!\top}\big(\mathbf a_{t+i}-\mathbf x_P(t)\big)a~t+i​=R(θg​)⊤(at+i​−xP​(t))
Action representation (goal frame)MLP-BCDiffusion
absolute target position26 %20 %
offset from the current pusher36 %46 %
One training sample: goal at the origin, 0°

Without the goal frame : 0 % success for both models — a good representation saves data.

Goal Frame: Equation ↔ Code

A rotation and a translation — written out by hand so it works on any batch shape

p~=R(θg) ⁣⊤(p−xg)=(cs−sc)v\tilde{\mathbf p}=R(\theta_g)^{\!\top}(\mathbf p-\mathbf x_g)=\begin{pmatrix}c&s\\-s&c\end{pmatrix}\mathbf vp~​=R(θg​)⊤(p−xg​)=(c−s​sc​)v
ot=(x~P, x~G, cos⁡θ~, sin⁡θ~)o_t=\big(\tilde{\mathbf x}_P,\ \tilde{\mathbf x}_G,\ \cos\tilde\theta,\ \sin\tilde\theta\big)ot​=(x~P​, x~G​, cosθ~, sinθ~)
a~=R(θg) ⁣⊤(a−xP),a=xP+R(θg)a~\tilde{\mathbf a}=R(\theta_g)^{\!\top}(\mathbf a-\mathbf x_P),\quad \mathbf a=\mathbf x_P+R(\theta_g)\tilde{\mathbf a}a~=R(θg​)⊤(a−xP​),a=xP​+R(θg​)a~

State layout: s=(xP,yP,xG,yG,θ,xg,yg,θg)s=(x_P,y_P,x_G,y_G,\theta,x_g,y_g,\theta_g)s=(xP​,yP​,xG​,yG​,θ,xg​,yg​,θg​) → indices 0–7.

pusht_imitation.py : to_goal_frame · make_obs · action_to_model

def to_goal_frame(points, state, origin=None):

c, s = math.cos(state[7]), math.sin(state[7])

v = points - (state[5:7] if origin is None else origin)

return np.stack([ c * v[..., 0] + s * v[..., 1],

-s * v[..., 0] + c * v[..., 1]], -1)

def make_obs(state):

p = to_goal_frame(state[0:2], state)

T = to_goal_frame(state[2:4], state)

dth = state[4] - state[7]

return np.array([p[0], p[1], T[0], T[1],

math.cos(dth), math.sin(dth)])

def action_to_model(actions, state):

return to_goal_frame(actions, state, origin=state[0:2])

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
19

Observation History and Action Chunks

See two frames, plan eight actions, execute four, then re-plan

Observe(ot−1, ot)(o_{t-1},\,o_t)(ot−1​,ot​), To=2T_o=2To​=2
Predictat:t+8\mathbf a_{t:t+8}at:t+8​, H=8H=8H=8 (0.8 s)
Executefirst Ta=4T_a=4Ta​=4 actions
Re-planreceding horizon

Two frames : the difference gives velocity — is the T still sliding?

Chunks : one-step predictions change every 0.1 s and jitter; a chunk commits to a motion for a moment.

Normalize : z=(x−μ)/σz=(x-\mu)/\sigmaz=(x−μ)/σ, statistics from the training set only.

Split by episode : neighbouring steps are nearly identical — a step-wise split leaks data into validation.

Trade-off : 4 steps run open-loop, so an unexpected slide is corrected only at the next re-plan.

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
20

Which Model? The Multimodality Problem

MSE learns the average action — the average of two good actions can be bad

min⁡ϕ E(o,a)∼D∥πϕ(o)−a∥2⟹π∗(o)=E[a∣o]\min_\phi\ \mathbb E_{(o,a)\sim\mathcal D}\big\lVert\pi_\phi(o)-a\big\rVert^2\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \pi^{*}(o)=\mathbb E[a\mid o]ϕmin​ E(o,a)∼D​​πϕ​(o)−a​2⟹π∗(o)=E[a∣o]
Toy problem: demonstrations avoid an obstacle up or down (50/50). MLP-BC learns a≈0a\approx0a≈0 — straight into it; Diffusion samples both modes.

In PushT : "go around the left or the right side of the T?" — MLP-BC hesitates in between; human demos are even more multimodal.

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab

Policy Model Candidates

Learn a distribution p(a∣o)p(a\mid o)p(a∣o) instead of a single average action

ModelOutputMultimodalNotes
MLP-BC (MSE)one pointno (mean)fastest, simplest — baseline
MLP-BC + action chunkHHH pointsnotemporally consistent
GMM / MDNKKK Gaussianspartlynumber of modes KKK fixed in advance
VQ-BeTdiscrete code + offsetyesTransformer
ACT (CVAE + Transformer)chunkyesreal robot, 50 demos
Diffusion Policychunk, sampledyesstandard PushT baseline, stable training

Our choice : MLP-BC as the baseline, Diffusion Policy as the main model — same MLP trunk (3 layers, width 512, Mish).

So the only difference is : learning the mean vs. learning the distribution.

Diffusion Policy (DDPM) in Three Steps

Learn to remove noise from action chunks; at run time, denoise noise into actions

1. Forward process: mix noise into a clean chunk a0\mathbf a^0a0

ak=αˉk a0+1−αˉk ϵ,ϵ∼N(0,I),k=1,…,K\mathbf a^k=\sqrt{\bar\alpha_k}\,\mathbf a^0+\sqrt{1-\bar\alpha_k}\,\boldsymbol\epsilon,\qquad \boldsymbol\epsilon\sim\mathcal N(\mathbf 0,I),\quad k=1,\dots,Kak=αˉk​​a0+1−αˉk​​ϵ,ϵ∼N(0,I),k=1,…,K

2. Training: predict the noise

L=Ek,ϵ∥ϵϕ(ak,k,o)−ϵ∥2\mathcal L=\mathbb E_{k,\boldsymbol\epsilon}\big\lVert\epsilon_\phi(\mathbf a^k,k,o)-\boldsymbol\epsilon\big\rVert^2L=Ek,ϵ​​ϵϕ​(ak,k,o)−ϵ​2

3. Sampling: start from aK∼N(0,I)\mathbf a^K\sim\mathcal N(\mathbf 0,I)aK∼N(0,I), step k=K,…,1k=K,\dots,1k=K,…,1

a^0=ak−1−αˉk ϵϕαˉk,ak−1=αˉk−1 βk1−αˉka^0+αk (1−αˉk−1)1−αˉkak+σkz\hat{\mathbf a}^0=\frac{\mathbf a^k-\sqrt{1-\bar\alpha_k}\,\epsilon_\phi}{\sqrt{\bar\alpha_k}},\qquad \mathbf a^{k-1}=\frac{\sqrt{\bar\alpha_{k-1}}\,\beta_k}{1-\bar\alpha_k}\hat{\mathbf a}^0+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha_k}\,(1-\bar\alpha_{k-1})}{1-\bar\alpha_k}\mathbf a^k+\sigma_k\mathbf za^0=αˉk​​ak−1−αˉk​​ϵϕ​​,ak−1=1−αˉk​αˉk−1​​βk​​a^0+1−αˉk​αk​​(1−αˉk−1​)​ak+σk​z

Why it is multimodal : each run starts from different noise → a different sample → different modes of the demonstrations. Cosine schedule βk\beta_kβk​, K=50K=50K=50.

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
23

Diffusion Policy: Equation ↔ Code

Training is one line of noise mixing; sampling is a loop of K = 50 denoising steps

Training

ak=αˉk a0+1−αˉk ϵ\mathbf a^k=\sqrt{\bar\alpha_k}\,\mathbf a^0+\sqrt{1-\bar\alpha_k}\,\boldsymbol\epsilonak=αˉk​​a0+1−αˉk​​ϵ
L=∥ϵϕ(ak,k,o)−ϵ∥2\mathcal L=\lVert\epsilon_\phi(\mathbf a^k,k,o)-\boldsymbol\epsilon\rVert^2L=∥ϵϕ​(ak,k,o)−ϵ∥2

Sampling

a^0=ak−1−αˉk ϵϕαˉk\hat{\mathbf a}^0=\frac{\mathbf a^k-\sqrt{1-\bar\alpha_k}\,\epsilon_\phi}{\sqrt{\bar\alpha_k}}a^0=αˉk​​ak−1−αˉk​​ϵϕ​​
ak−1=μk(a^0,ak)+σkz\mathbf a^{k-1}=\mu_k(\hat{\mathbf a}^0,\mathbf a^k)+\sigma_k\mathbf zak−1=μk​(a^0,ak)+σk​z

pusht_imitation.py : DiffusionPolicy.loss · predict

def loss(self, x, y):

a0 = self.ny(y)

k = torch.randint(0, self.K, (len(a0),))

eps = torch.randn_like(a0)

ab = self.abar[k][:, None]

a_k = ab.sqrt() * a0 + (1 - ab).sqrt() * eps

return ((self.eps_hat(a_k, k, self.nx(x)) - eps) ** 2).mean()

def predict(self, x):

a = torch.randn(len(xn), self.d_act) # a^K ~ N(0, I)

for k in reversed(range(self.K)):

ab = self.abar[k]

a0 = (a - (1 - ab).sqrt() * self.eps_hat(a, kk, xn)) / ab.sqrt()

mean = (ab_prev.sqrt() * self.betas[k] / (1 - ab)) * a0 \

+ (self.alphas[k].sqrt() * (1 - ab_prev) / (1 - ab)) * a

var = self.betas[k] * (1 - ab_prev) / (1 - ab)

a = mean + var.sqrt() * torch.randn_like(a)

Training Settings Found by Experiment

Diffusion needs more capacity or more steps than MLP-BC before it pays off

Setting (180 expert demos, unseen tasks)MLP-BCDiffusion
width 256, lr 10−310^{-3}10−3, no EMA, 30k steps32 %8 %
width 256, lr 3×10−33\times10^{-3}3×10−3 + EMA, 30k steps36–46 %18–42 %
width 256, lr 3×10−33\times10^{-3}3×10−3 + EMA, 80k steps—64 %
width 512, lr 3×10−33\times10^{-3}3×10−3 + EMA, 30k steps (default)38–59 %54–63 %
width 512, lr 3×10−33\times10^{-3}3×10−3 + EMA, 50k steps—69 %
ϕEMA←0.999 ϕEMA+0.001 ϕ\phi_{\text{EMA}}\leftarrow 0.999\,\phi_{\text{EMA}}+0.001\,\phiϕEMA​←0.999ϕEMA​+0.001ϕ

Batch 256, AdamW, cosine lr decay; about 1 min per model on GPU, 4 min on CPU.

Caution : re-running the same setting moves success by ±10 %; validation loss does not predict success (0.116 → 0.132 while 62 % → 69 %).

How Many Demonstrations? Measured

Diffusion reaches most of its performance at about 100 demonstrations

Experiment

400 expert demos → train on the first NNN → width 512, 30k steps → 100 unseen tasks, closed loop.

Reading

Diffusion is ahead at every NNN, most clearly with little data.

MLP-BC needs about 3× more demos for the same success.

Human demos : aim for at least 100, ideally 200.

Reproduce : tools/imitation_experiments/full/run_scale512.sh (10 jobs, about 15 min)

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
26

Closed-Loop Rollouts and Failure Modes

Both learned policies stay below the expert that taught them

MLP-BC rollouts on unseen seeds
Diffusion Policy rollouts on the same seeds

MLP-BC : stalls or jitters beside the T — it averages between strategies.

Diffusion : commits to one strategy; fails at the final 0.15 m / 10° alignment or after one big mistake.

Both : covariate shift — their own errors lead to states never seen in the demos.

Default run (180 demos) : expert 88 % · MLP-BC 38–59 % · Diffusion 54–63 %. Remedy: DAgger — record human corrections.

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
27

From Imitation to Reinforcement Learning

RL needs no demonstrations — only a reward and a great deal of trial and error

st→ πϕ(a∣s) at→ physics st+1, rt,max⁡ϕ E[∑tγtrt]s_t\xrightarrow{\ \pi_\phi(a\mid s)\ }a_t\xrightarrow{\ \text{physics}\ }s_{t+1},\ r_t,\qquad \max_\phi\ \mathbb E\Big[\sum_t\gamma^t r_t\Big]st​ πϕ​(a∣s) ​at​ physics ​st+1​, rt​,ϕmax​ E[t∑​γtrt​]
ExperimentStarting pointResult (50 unseen tasks, no exploration noise)
A. RL from scratchrandom network16.4M steps (2.5 h): 0 % success
B. BC → RLMLP-BC policy42 % → 74 % (3M) → 80 % (6M, 80 min)
Scripted experthand-written rules88 %

Why A fails : success needs a long sequence — choose a contact, go around, push, align. Random motion almost never completes it, so the success bonus is never observed (the exploration problem).

Why B works : imitation already succeeds about half the time; RL only has to polish behaviour that works.

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
28

Reward Design

Turn "how far is the task from done" into one number, and reward every step of progress

Remaining cost (1 rad ≈ 0.3 m)

c(s)=∥xG−xg∥+0.3 ∣θ−θg∣c(s)=\lVert\mathbf x_G-\mathbf x_g\rVert+0.3\,\lvert\theta-\theta_g\rvertc(s)=∥xG​−xg​∥+0.3∣θ−θg​∣

Reward per step

rt=10(c(st)−c(st+1))⏟progress−0.02max⁡(∥xP−xG∥−1,0)⏟far from T−0.01⏟time+10⋅1[success]⏟bonusr_t=\underbrace{10\big(c(s_t)-c(s_{t+1})\big)}_{\text{progress}}-\underbrace{0.02\max(\lVert\mathbf x_P-\mathbf x_G\rVert-1,0)}_{\text{far from T}}-\underbrace{0.01}_{\text{time}}+\underbrace{10\cdot\mathbb 1[\text{success}]}_{\text{bonus}}rt​=progress10(c(st​)−c(st+1​))​​−far from T0.02max(∥xP​−xG​∥−1,0)​​−time0.01​​+bonus10⋅1[success]​​

Potential-based shaping : the progress term sums to 10 (c(s0)−c(sT))10\,(c(s_0)-c(s_T))10(c(s0​)−c(sT​)) — a signal every step, without changing the optimal policy.

Episode end : success is terminal; at 300 steps it is truncated and bootstrapped with V(sT)V(s_T)V(sT​).

Side effect : the time penalty makes RL finish faster than the expert, which has no such pressure.

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab

Reward: Equation ↔ Code

Two short functions — the RL training loop calls them once per environment step

c(s)=∥xG−xg∥+0.3 ∣θ−θg∣c(s)=\lVert\mathbf x_G-\mathbf x_g\rVert+0.3\,\lvert\theta-\theta_g\rvertc(s)=∥xG​−xg​∥+0.3∣θ−θg​∣
rt=10 Δc−0.02max⁡(∥xP−xG∥−1,0)r_t=10\,\Delta c-0.02\max(\lVert\mathbf x_P-\mathbf x_G\rVert-1,0)rt​=10Δc−0.02max(∥xP​−xG​∥−1,0)
− 0.01+10⋅1[success]\qquad-\,0.01+10\cdot\mathbb 1[\text{success}]−0.01+10⋅1[success]

Δc=c(st)−c(st+1)\Delta c=c(s_t)-c(s_{t+1})Δc=c(st​)−c(st+1​) : positive when the T moved closer to the goal pose.

wrap_angle keeps θ−θg\theta-\theta_gθ−θg​ in (−π,π](-\pi,\pi](−π,π], so 359∘359^\circ359∘ counts as 1∘1^\circ1∘.

pusht_rl.py : task_cost · reward

def task_cost(sim):

return float(np.linalg.norm(sim.T.pos - sim.goal_pos)

+ 0.3 * abs(wrap_angle(sim.T.angle - sim.goal_angle)))

def reward(c_before, c_after, sim, success):

dist_pt = float(np.linalg.norm(sim.pusher.pos - sim.T.pos))

return (10.0 * (c_before - c_after)

- 0.02 * max(dist_pt - 1.0, 0.0)

- 0.01

+ (10.0 if success else 0.0))

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab

PPO: Small, Clipped Policy Updates

Collect 8,192 steps, estimate advantages, update for 10 epochs — repeat

Advantage (GAE), γ=0.99, λ=0.95\gamma=0.99,\ \lambda=0.95γ=0.99, λ=0.95

δt=rt+γV(st+1)−V(st),A^t=∑l≥0(γλ)lδt+l\delta_t=r_t+\gamma V(s_{t+1})-V(s_t),\quad \hat A_t=\sum_{l\ge0}(\gamma\lambda)^l\delta_{t+l}δt​=rt​+γV(st+1​)−V(st​),A^t​=l≥0∑​(γλ)lδt+l​

Clipped objective, ρ=πϕ/πold\rho=\pi_\phi/\pi_{\text{old}}ρ=πϕ​/πold​

L=−E[min⁡(ρA^, clip(ρ,0.8,1.2)A^)]L=-\mathbb E\big[\min\big(\rho\hat A,\ \mathrm{clip}(\rho,0.8,1.2)\hat A\big)\big]L=−E[min(ρA^, clip(ρ,0.8,1.2)A^)]

Gaussian policy N(μϕ(s),σ2)\mathcal N(\mu_\phi(s),\sigma^2)N(μϕ​(s),σ2): sample while training, use the mean μϕ\mu_\phiμϕ​ for evaluation.

A. scratchB. BC → RL
Actor2 × 256 tanhthe MLP-BC net
Initial σ\sigmaσ0.60.03 m
Actor lr3×10−43\times10^{-4}3×10−43×10−53\times10^{-5}3×10−5
Criticnewnew, 10 warm-up updates

For B : clipping, a small learning rate and small noise protect the working BC policy from being destroyed early on.

PPO: Equation ↔ Code

GAE runs backwards through the rollout; the clipped loss is one line

Advantage, backwards in time

δt=rt+γV(st+1)(1−dt)−V(st)\delta_t=r_t+\gamma V(s_{t+1})(1-d_t)-V(s_t)δt​=rt​+γV(st+1​)(1−dt​)−V(st​)
A^t=δt+γλ(1−dt)A^t+1\hat A_t=\delta_t+\gamma\lambda(1-d_t)\hat A_{t+1}A^t​=δt​+γλ(1−dt​)A^t+1​

Clipped surrogate

L=−E[min⁡(ρA^, clip(ρ,0.8,1.2)A^)]L=-\mathbb E\big[\min(\rho\hat A,\ \mathrm{clip}(\rho,0.8,1.2)\hat A)\big]L=−E[min(ρA^, clip(ρ,0.8,1.2)A^)]
ρ=exp⁡(log⁡πϕ(a∣s)−log⁡πold(a∣s))\rho=\exp\big(\log\pi_\phi(a\mid s)-\log\pi_{\text{old}}(a\mid s)\big)ρ=exp(logπϕ​(a∣s)−logπold​(a∣s))

pusht_rl.py : train loop

for t in reversed(range(n_steps)): # GAE

nv = v_next if t == n_steps - 1 else V[t + 1]

delta = R[t] + gamma * nv * (1 - DONE[t]) - V[t]

g = delta + gamma * lam * (1 - DONE[t]) * g

adv[t] = g

RET = ADV + torch.cat(V) # critic target

for _ in range(10): # PPO epochs

...

lv = ((model.value(Ob[idx]) - RET[idx]) ** 2).mean()

if it >= critic_warmup:

ratio = (policy_dist(model, Ob[idx]).log_prob(Ab[idx])

.sum(-1) - LPb[idx]).exp()

la = -torch.min(ratio * ADV[idx],

ratio.clamp(0.8, 1.2) * ADV[idx]).mean()

RL from Scratch vs BC → RL

Starting from imitation turns an unsolvable exploration problem into fine-tuning

Training success with exploration noise (tutorial/checkpoints/rl/*/train_log*.txt)

From scratch : return improved −14 → −2.4, the T moves toward the goal, but 0 % success; exploration noise collapsed 0.6 → 0.03.

BC → RL : 0.44 → 0.88 in 6M steps, then a plateau at 0.87–0.90.

Lesson : demonstrations solve exploration; RL then optimizes what imitation cannot — the reward itself.

Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
35

Why BC+PPO Stops Near the Expert

More training made the policy faster, not more successful

Measured

Within noise : at 82 %, one standard error is about ±4 points with 100 tasks.

6M → 12M : success 82 % → 77 %, mean time 12.0 s → 11.1 s.

Plateau : training success stayed at 0.87–0.90 with noise σ→0.005\sigma\to0.005σ→0.005.

Best checkpoint : 6M, not the newest 12M.

Likely structural limits (not yet tested)

Less information : 12 inputs vs. the expert's full state and memory.

Conservative updates : lr 3×10−53\times10^{-5}3×10−5, clipping, small σ\sigmaσ.

Reward ≠ success test : angle weighted 0.3; success needs 0.15 m and 10°.

One step at a time : BC drops 54 % → 34 % without chunks.

Not a ceiling : RL optimizes the reward, not imitation — align the reward with the success test to push further.

Summary

Physics gives the data, imitation gives the start, RL gives the polish

  • Imitation learning = supervised learning on (ot,at:t+H)(o_t,\mathbf a_{t:t+H})(ot​,at:t+H​) from successful demonstrations
    • Goal frame (relative motion) and action offsets matter as much as the model
  • Data: about 100 demos for Diffusion Policy; collect 100–200 human demos
  • Model: MSE learns E[a∣o]\mathbb E[a\mid o]E[a∣o]; Diffusion learns the distribution and handles multimodality
    • Measured: Diffusion 20–68 % vs MLP-BC 9–59 % (25–360 demos)
  • RL from scratch: 0 % after 16M steps — the exploration problem
    • BC → PPO: 54 % → 82 % on seeds 0–99, tied with the expert (87 %) and 3–4 s faster
Sookmyung Women’s Univ. Autonomous Mechanical Systems Lab
38

Try It Yourself

Exercises with the tutorial code

  • Human data : collect 50, then 100 demos — does the Diffusion vs MLP-BC gap grow?
  • No goal frame : train in world coordinates — how much success is lost?
  • Chunks : vary the prediction / execution horizons (1, 4, 8, 16)
  • History : one observation frame — which mistakes appear without velocity?
  • DAgger : record your corrections in the viewer and retrain
  • Robustness : evaluate with friction 0.2 instead of 0.4 — think domain randomization
  • RL : put the 0.15 m / 10° test into the reward — does BC+PPO pass the expert?
